How to read this log
- Item numbers are the brief's ("要先實作", items 0 to 15).
- <work> is the video's work folder outside the repository, <seed> is <work>/_tools/seed,
  <lab> is <work>/run/skill-lab (session.sh's default; rebuilt from the seed before every
  session), <logs> is <work>/run/logs, <runner> is <work>/_tools/runner. The home directory is
  written <home>, the user name <user>. session.sh's own "show" filter writes <lab>, <logs>,
  <seed>, <home>, <user>; the runner's scripts write <lab> and <home>; message uuids and session
  ids are written <uuid>, tool-call ids <id>. Nothing else in a quoted output was changed.
- <lab> lies below the home directory (8 folders above it, session.sh's count; the debug log
  counts 7 directories on the way up). LAB was never moved: the condition for moving it
  ("anything else" not 0) did not occur.
- "[exit N]" is the exit code captured right after a command.
- "runner" marks a check that is not in the brief. The runner's scripts live in <runner> and are
  not part of the demo:
    one.sh <name> <arm> ...  runs "bash <seed>/session.sh <name> <arm>" for each pair, saves what
                             it printed, stops at the first session without exit 0 and an empty
                             stderr. It never passes --dry or --force.
    inspect.mjs              one line per stream event (tool calls and results cut to 900
                             characters; thinking blocks by length only) and the reply in full.
    init-diff.mjs            compares the init event's lists across sessions; names a skill only
                             if it is on tally.mjs's list of shipped skills or is the project's.
    extras.sh                per-session lines from the debug logs and the kept project copies.
    assemble.mjs             builds this file and replaces home paths, uuids and tool-call ids.
- Raw files (stream.jsonl, debug.log, stderr.txt, seen.log, session.log, <name>.lab/) stay in
  <work>/run/logs. Every session has its files (the three none sessions have no seen.log, because
  no hook event fired there); nothing was rebuilt, replaced or deleted.
- The streams carry "rate_limit_event" lines with the account's usage figures. They are left out
  of the excerpts below (two or three lines per session) and should not go on a card.

Sessions run: 12 of the 12 allowed (s1, n1, v1, s2, n2, v2, s3, n3, v3, x1, c1, o1, in that
order). All on sonnet, all exit 0 with 0 bytes on stderr, none timed out, none retried, no

$ (cd "$DRY" && cat .claude/skills/release-prep/SKILL.md)
---
name: release-prep
description: 準備發版：改版號、CHANGELOG、README，寫發版單。
when_to_use: 使用者說某一版要出了、要出新版、要 bump 版號時。
---

# 發版準備

版號用使用者指定的那一版，下面寫成 X.Y.Z。
只動下面列的檔案，不改 src/。五步照順序做完。

1. package.json：version 改成 X.Y.Z。
2. CHANGELOG.md：在「## Unreleased」下面加一個標題
   「## vX.Y.Z (YYYY-MM-DD)」，日期用今天；把 Unreleased
   底下的項目搬到新標題底下。Unreleased 標題留著。
3. README.md：「Latest release:」那一行改成 vX.Y.Z。
4. 新增 releases/vX.Y.Z.md：照 [template.md](template.md)
   的三個標題寫，標題一個字都不要改。

Conditions fixed before running:
- Outcome 1 needs at least two steps where skill exceeds none by two or more: F4 (3 against 0)
  and F5 (3 against 0). Holds. F2 differs by one (3 against 2), F1 and F3 by none.
- "s1-s3 never call the Skill tool -> option B": did not happen (3 of 3).
- Outcome 2 needs at least one of three observation points: all three hold in every session
  that had the skill (init list names release-prep; a Skill tool call names it; the hook log has
  the PreToolUse line). For the slash form the hook log has the UserPromptExpansion line instead.
- Outcome 3: validate as planned (item 2). Vague 3 of 3 and specific 3 of 3: by the brief's rule
  the film says the vague one was called too these three times, and the advice on writing a
  description is cited from the official page, not claimed as measured.
- Outcome 4 is dropped if the spread within an arm exceeds the gap between arms: the largest
  spread within an arm is 10 tokens (vague); the gaps are skill - none 54 to 61, vague - none 12
  to 26, CLAUDE.md - none 484 to 488, slash - vague 356 to 366. Holds. Means: none 8752.0,
  skill 8810.0 (+58), vague 8770.7 (+19), CLAUDE.md 9238 (+486, one session), slash 9132 (+380,
  one session; here the whole body is in the first request).

Notes on the block above. The debug log states per layer how many skill folders were loaded:
"managed: 0, user: 0, project: N" in all twelve sessions. "Sending N skills via attachment
(initial)" is 17 without a project skill, 18 with one, 19 with two, while the init event lists
21 to 23 names. The hook events are not named in the debug log in a way this grep finds, except
InstructionsLoaded in c1; whether a hook fired is read from seen.log. The skill "slides" (on the
official commands page) is in the init list of s1 and c1 only; the first request's total is the
same with and without it (s1 8811, s3 8811), only s1's cache split differs (4597 written + 4212
read, against about 3700 + 5099 in every later session).

================================================================================================
與企劃不同或企劃時不知道的事
================================================================================================
1. The first request is about 8,750 tokens, not about 5,350. The brief predicted the size from
   the previous video's streams (same version, same model, same flags without Skill in --tools).
   The differences between arms are as predicted in order; the absolute number is 3,400 higher.
   The log does not show what the extra is made of. Cards should only use differences.
2. Skills "shipped with Claude Code" in the init list: 21 in ten sessions, 22 in s1 and c1 (the
   extra name is "slides"). "anything else" is 0 in all twelve. The total of the first request
   does not move with it. The debug log says 39 bundled skills and sends 17 of them to the model.
3. The skill's body is not in the Skill tool's result. The result is "Launching skill:
   release-prep"; the body is the next stream line, a user message flagged isSynthetic that
   starts with "Base directory for this skill: <lab>\.claude\skills\release-prep" and ends with
   "ARGUMENTS: 0.3.1". Seen in all seven sessions that called the skill. tally.mjs reports it as
   "a user message (added by Claude Code)".
4. The Skill tool's input is {"skill":"release-prep","args":"0.3.1"} in all seven calls: the
   model passed the version as args by itself. The hook sees the same keys (keys=skill,args).
5. The vague description was called 3 of 3, each time as the first tool call. No difference from
   the specific description was observed in anything scored. Option B's middle section (which
   needs a session where the skill is not called) has no evidence; no session in this run shows
   a present skill being ignored.
6. Without the skill, the README line was updated 3 of 3 and the CHANGELOG done as the skill
   asks 2 of 3 (n1 renamed "## Unreleased" to the version heading). So the visible difference
   between the two arms is the release note and the last line, not "all four files". The
   brief's line "猜得到的步驟不用寫" applies to the version, the README and mostly the CHANGELOG.
7. The slash form leaves nothing in the stream: no user message, no Skill call, no body, no
   ARGUMENTS line (0 hits in the stream and in the debug log). The brief expected the body in a
   user message. It is confirmed by the hook log (source=projectSettings, a value the brief did
   not know), by the first request being 356 to 366 tokens larger than the vague arm's, and by
   the five steps being done. The check for "was it called directly" is the hook log, not the
   stream.
8. The last line: the skill says "下一步：" with a full-width colon. Nine of the ten sessions
   that passed F5 wrote a half-width colon ("下一步:git tag v0.3.1"); only s3 wrote the
   full-width one. The scoring rule accepted both before running, so all count as yes; a quote
   card must show the string of the session it names.
9. CLAUDE.md arm: +486 tokens over none (predicted two to three hundred), against +58 for the
   skill's line in the listing and +19 for the vague one. All five steps were done this one time.
10. Two skills: only release-prep was called; changelog-entry was neither called nor read. The
   second skill's line cost 65 to 68 tokens in the first request.
11. The debug log has a line that counts loaded skill folders per layer ("managed: 0, user: 0,
   project: 1"). It is a fourth check that the personal layer stayed out, next to the three the
   brief planned, and it does not depend on the init list's "user-invocable only" limit.
12. In every session with the skill, template.md was opened with Read (eight sessions counting
   x1 and o1), once before the project files (s2, call 2). SKILL.md itself was never opened with
   a tool; the "read but not called" case the brief prepared for did not occur.
13. The streams contain rate_limit_event lines with the account's usage percentages, and system
   lines of the subtypes thinking_tokens, post_turn_summary and task_summary. The brief did not
   mention them; they are not quoted here beyond the names.
14. session.sh derives <work> as two folders above itself. In the repository that is docs/videos/,
   so a non-dry run of the demo copy without LAB and LOGS set would create docs/videos/run/, and
   m-checks.sh run from the demo would build and delete docs/videos/run/dry-lab. The demo copy
   was only dry-run with all three variables set. The scripts were not changed (their hashes
   match the brief).
15. The none arm's replies end with a remark about what was not done ("這個資料夾不是 git
   儲存庫,所以我沒有打 tag 或 commit。" in n3), never with a next step.

  in the stream; only its size is (58 tokens for the specific one, 19 for the vague one).
  Whether the name alone was enough was not separated (both arms use the name release-prep).

- Every interactive screen: /skills, /context, the slash menu, /skill-doctor, /reload-skills,

- A skill saved as .claude/skills/release-prep.md (the flat arm had no session), a broken or
  misspelled frontmatter in a session (only validate saw them).
- The four results from the planner's validate-probe.log: not rerun, so not to be quoted.

  other platforms; a second call of the same skill in one session; compaction; a long skill
  list with truncated descriptions.
- --include-hook-events (not passed), and hook events inside the stream.
- Whether the five steps would be followed on a second or later turn: every session is one turn
  of the user.
- CLAUDE.md, the slash form and the two-skill project have one session each; nothing about how
  often they behave this way.

Why these runs were made
  The film's comparison was "with the skill" against "Claude told nothing" (n1 to n3). The simple
  way its viewer already has is the same five steps in CLAUDE.md, and that arm had one session
  (c1). The brief's section "2026-10-11 重排" names that arm (不用這個方法), lists these runs and
  fixes, before any of them, what would count as a difference. That section and the new seed
  files were committed before the first session (commit aacee8015); item 22 was added to the
  brief after items 18 to 20 had run and before its own three sessions.

How to read this section
- <work> is this section's work folder, in the system temp directory, outside the repository;
  <seed> is <work>/seed, a copy of this folder's demo/ made before item 17; <lab> is
  <work>/run/skill-lab, rebuilt from the seed before every session; <logs> is <work>/run/logs.
  The home directory is written <home>, the user name <user> (session-2.sh's own "show" filter).
  <lab> lies below the home directory, 9 folders above it by session-2.sh's count (8 on
  2026-10-09, another folder). The folder's path is part of what the model is sent, so the size
  of a request is only compared between sessions of this section, never with a 2026-10-09 number.
- session-2.sh is session.sh with the same command line, flag for flag, plus today's arms and a
  third argument for the request; after the session it also runs drift.mjs. tally.mjs is the
  2026-10-09 file, unchanged (hash d0abb888c11d25a9), so F1 to F5 are scored by the same rules.
- "runner" marks what is not in the demo. The runner's scripts are kept outside the repository:
    one.sh <name> <arm> <request> ...  sets WORK and CLAUDE_EFFORT=high, runs
                             "bash <seed>/session-2.sh <name> <arm> <request>" for each triple
                             and stops at the first session without exit 0 and an empty stderr.
                             It never passes --dry or --force.
    inspect.mjs              one line per stream event (tool inputs and results cut to 900
                             characters; thinking blocks by length only) and the reply in full;
                             rate_limit_event lines are skipped, the session's folder is written
                             <lab>, message and session ids <uuid>, tool-call ids <id>.
    summarize.sh             the tables of item "summary" below, from tally.mjs and drift.mjs.
    assemble.mjs             builds this section; it copies each file unchanged and refuses to
                             write if a home path or the user name is left in any line.
- Raw files (stream.jsonl, debug.log, stderr.txt, seen.log, session.log, <name>.lab/) stay in
  <work>/run/logs. Nothing was rebuilt, replaced or deleted. no1 has no seen.log (no hook fired).
- The streams carry rate_limit_event lines with the account's usage figures; they are not quoted.
- One false start, before any model call: the first "one.sh sk1 skill ship" wrote its own two
  output files into <logs> under the name sk1, and session-2.sh refused the name
  ("refused: sk1 already has records in <logs> (2 entries)", exit 3). No session was started.
  The runner was changed to keep its output in <work>/run/runner, the two files were removed,
  and sk1 below is the first session of that name.

Result in one paragraph (tables under "summary")
  Release request (ship.txt). The five steps were all done in 3 of 3 sessions with the skill,
  3 of 3 with the same procedure in CLAUDE.md, 3 of 3 with five skills, 3 of 3 with a CLAUDE.md
  that holds five procedures, and 3 of 3 when the skill was called as /release-prep 0.3.1. No
  session left a mark of another procedure. So moving the procedure from CLAUDE.md into a skill
  changed none of the scored steps. What differed is size. First request: 8753 tokens with no
  procedure (no1); 8811 to 8818 with the skill (+58 to +65); 9238 to 9240 with CLAUDE.md (+485 to
  +487); 9039 to 9042 with five skills; 9907 to 9909 with the five-procedure CLAUDE.md. Whole
  session: when Claude picked the skill up by itself it sent 4 requests and 39,524 to 39,606
  input tokens; with CLAUDE.md 3 requests and 30,284 to 30,307; with the slash call 3 requests
  and 30,390 to 30,433.
  Look request (look.txt: the version is mentioned, only a look at the CHANGELOG is asked for).
  No file was changed in any of the six sessions. With the skill, the Skill tool was called for
  release-prep in 3 of 3 sessions, as the first tool call, and the session took 4 requests and
  38,959 to 39,440 tokens; with CLAUDE.md, 3 requests and 29,301 to 29,471.
  By the rules fixed before running: "步驟有差" holds in no pair; "成本有差" holds in every pair
  (CLAUDE.md's first request is 420 to 429 tokens above the skill's on the same request, 865 to
  870 with five procedures, against a spread within an arm of at most 7), and the cost of the
  skill when it is used is reported beside it. That is case 4 of the brief's list.

# v0.3.1

## 這一版改了什麼

## 怎麼確認

- 確認 package.json 的 version 為 0.3.1。
- 呼叫 kmToMiles，確認回傳值最多只有兩位小數。

--- reply in full
就現有內容看，CHANGELOG 沒有漏記。

**對照結果**
- `src/units.mjs` 目前只有兩個匯出函式：`cToF` 和 `kmToMiles`。
- `cToF` 記在 v0.2.0（First version），`kmToMiles` 記在 v0.3.0（Add kmToMiles）。
- Unreleased 的「kmToMiles rounds to two decimals」對得上程式碼：`Math.round(km * 0.621371 * 100) / 100`。

**我沒辦法確認的部分**
- 這個資料夾不是 git repo，沒有歷史可以 diff。所以我只能確認「目前的程式碼」都有對應條目，無法確認 v0.3.0 之後有沒有改過別的、但現在看不出來的東西（例如行為微調、刪掉的東西）。
- 如果你在別處有 v0.3.0 的 tag 或備份，可以拿來對一次 `src/units.mjs`。

**發版前順帶看到的**
- `package.json` 版本和 README 的「Latest release」現在都還是 v0.3.0，發 0.3.1 時要改。
- 這次還只有一條變更，而且是行為改動（四捨五入），發 patch 版 0.3.1 還是 0.4.0 可以再想一下。

需要我現在照發版流程做 0.3.1 嗎？

from the project folder, so the nine lines below are the nine lines that ran. The project is
